 I_j\le \frac jt I_t,
 \qquad I_{j+1}-I_j\ge\frac{I_j}{j}
 \quad(1\le j<t).$$ Since $Q$ has density $1/Z$ on the success event, <a id="eq:entropy-total"></a>$$\label{eq:entropy-total}
 I_t=\log(1/Z)\le \log(1/v)+tL-\log b_0,
 \qquad L=\log(1/p_*)>0.$$

<a id="eq:entropy-prefix-lower"></a>
Take $t\ge4$, set $j=\floor{t/2}$ and $m=t-j$, and replace the last $m$ predicates successively by $\mathbf1$. If $W$ changes from one to zero during this procedure, at least one of the lifted comparisons to $\mathbf1$ fails. At each step the other coordinates are independent mixture draws or already fixed constant-one predicates. Averaging Equation <a href="#eq:entropy-lifted-order" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:entropy-lifted-order">[eq:entropy-lifted-order]</a> and then taking a union bound gives $$\delta:=Q\left(W\left(\bigwedge_{i=1}^jR(Y_i)\right)=0\right)
 \le \frac{m\varepsilon v}{Z}
 \le\frac{t\varepsilon}{b_0p}.$$ Here and below, adjoining constant-one predicates does not change the argument of $W$. Averaging Equation <a href="#eq:entropy-pattern-upper" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:entropy-pattern-upper">[eq:entropy-pattern-upper]</a> over the first $j$ types gives $$P_*^j\left(W\left(\bigwedge_{i=1}^jR(Y_i)\right)=1\right)
 \le v\bigl(K_0e^{-jL}+\varepsilon\bigr).$$ Coarsen the relative entropy to this binary event. Explicitly, if its probabilities under $Q_j$ and $P_*^j$ are $q$ and $a$, respectively, then $q=1-\delta$ by the definition of $\delta$. Convexity on the event and its complement yields $$I_j\ge q\log\frac qa+(1-q)\log\frac{1-q}{1-a}
       \ge q\log(1/a)-\log2.$$ The last inequality uses the upper bound $\log2$ on binary entropy and $-\log(1-a)\ge0$; endpoint cases follow by limits. Substituting the lower bound on $\log(1/a)$ is valid even when that bound is negative, since its multiplier is exactly $q=1-\delta\ge0$. Thus, with $a_v=\log(1/v)$, $$\label{eq:entropy-prefix-lower}
 I_j\ge(1-\delta)
       \bigl(a_v+jL-\log(K_0+\varepsilon e^{jL})\bigr)-\log2.$$

<a id="eq:entropy-epsilon-small"></a>
<a id="eq:entropy-normalization-lower"></a>
<a id="eq:entropy-prefix-rate"></a>
Impose the explicit smallness condition $$\label{eq:entropy-epsilon-small}
 \frac{t^2\varepsilon}{p}\le c_0,
 \qquad c_0=\min\{b_0/8,1\}.$$ It implies $\delta\le1/(8t)$, $\delta jL\le L/16$, $1-\delta-j/t\ge3/8$, and $\varepsilon e^{jL}\le1$. Combining Equations <a href="#eq:entropy-convex-increments" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:entropy-convex-increments">[eq:entropy-convex-increments]</a>–<a href="#eq:entropy-prefix-lower" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:entropy-prefix-lower">[eq:entropy-prefix-lower]</a> gives $$(1-\delta-j/t)a_v
 \le\delta jL+(1-\delta)\log(K_0+\varepsilon e^{jL})
       +\log2-(j/t)\log b_0.$$ Define constants, independent of $t$ and all primitive supports, by $$\begin{aligned}
 R_0&=L/16+\log(K_0+1)+\log2+\tfrac12\abs{\log b_0},\\
 A_v&=\tfrac83 R_0,\qquad c_v=e^{-A_v},\qquad c_z=b_0c_v.
\end{aligned}$$ The preceding inequality implies $a_v\le A_v$, hence $$\label{eq:entropy-normalization-lower}
 v\ge c_v,\qquad Z\ge c_zp.$$ This derivation does not assume $v\le1$. The other hypothesis, $v\le B_0$, gives the separate bound $(1-\delta)a_v\ge-\max\{\log B_0,0\}$. Therefore Equation <a href="#eq:entropy-prefix-lower" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:entropy-prefix-lower">[eq:entropy-prefix-lower]</a> also gives $$\label{eq:entropy-prefix-rate}
 I_j\ge jL-C_I,
 \qquad
 C_I=L/16+\log(K_0+1)+\log2+\max\{\log B_0,0\}.$$ If $c_v>B_0$, the assumptions were inconsistent and there is nothing further to prove.

<a id="pm-h-34"></a>
###### A common conditional density and concentration of types.

<a id="eq:entropy-common-density"></a>
<a id="eq:entropy-next-lower"></a>
For a full ordered prefix $D=(Y_1,\ldots,Y_j)$, write $R_D=\bigwedge_{i=1}^jR(Y_i)$ and set $$\begin{aligned}
 Z_D&=\mathbb E_{P_*^m}W\left(R_D\wedge\bigwedge_{i=1}^mR(Y_i)\right),\\
 U_D(R)&=\mathbb E_{P_*^{m-1}}
          W\left(R_D\wedge R\wedge\bigwedge_{i=1}^{m-1}R(Y_i)\right).
\end{aligned}$$ The density of the $Q$-prefix law relative to $P_*^j$ is $Z_D/Z$. In particular $Z_D>0$ for $Q$-almost every prefix. On those prefixes define $$\label{eq:entropy-common-density}
 f_D(R)=\frac{U_D(R)}{Z_D}.$$ Then $f_D(R(Y))$ is the conditional next-coordinate density relative to $P_*$, so $$0\le f_D\le1/Z_D,\qquad \int f_D\,\mathrm dP_*=1.$$ Equation <a href="#eq:entropy-common-density" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:entropy-common-density">[eq:entropy-common-density]</a> defines its value in terms of the predicate alone, even for predicates with several presentations or occurring in several types. The entropy chain rule and Equations <a href="#eq:entropy-convex-increments" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:entropy-convex-increments">[eq:entropy-convex-increments]</a> and <a href="#eq:entropy-prefix-rate" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:entropy-prefix-rate">[eq:entropy-prefix-rate]</a> show that $$\label{eq:entropy-next-lower}
 \mathbb E_D\int f_D\log f_D\,\mathrm dP_*\ge L-C_I/j.$$

<a id="eq:entropy-type-domination"></a>
<a id="eq:entropy-type-conditional-rate"></a>
Put $$\pi_s=\frac{\omega_s p_s}{p_*},\qquad
 u=\frac{\varepsilon}{b_0p},\qquad
 \mu_s(D)=\omega_s\mathbb E_{P_s}f_D(R).$$ The vector $\pi$ is a probability vector, possibly with zero entries. For every full type sequence $\boldsymbol s$, Equations <a href="#eq:entropy-iid-success" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:entropy-iid-success">[eq:entropy-iid-success]</a> and <a href="#eq:entropy-pattern-upper" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:entropy-pattern-upper">[eq:entropy-pattern-upper]</a> give $$\label{eq:entropy-type-domination}
 Q\bigl(\text{types}=\boldsymbol s\bigr)
 \le\frac{K_0}{b_0}\prod_{i=1}^t\pi_{s_i}
      +u\prod_{i=1}^t\omega_{s_i}.$$ For a fixed $s$, let $F_s$ be its empirical frequency among the $m$ suffix coordinates. Under the product law $\pi^{\otimes t}$, $$\mathbb E\abs{F_s-\pi_s}
 \le\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(F_s)}
 =\sqrt{\frac{\pi_s(1-\pi_s)}m}\le\frac1{2\sqrt m}.$$ Under any law, $\abs{F_s-\pi_s}\le1$. Applying the domination in Equation <a href="#eq:entropy-type-domination" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:entropy-type-domination">[eq:entropy-type-domination]</a> to this nonnegative function therefore gives $\mathbb E_Q\abs{F_s-\pi_s}\le R_t$, where <a id="eq:entropy-type-rate"></a>$$\label{eq:entropy-type-rate}
 R_t=\frac{K_0}{2b_0\sqrt m}+u.$$ Fixing the entire ordered prefix $D$, including its predicates and any presentation marks, still leaves an exchangeable suffix: its reference law is a product, and its conditioning event depends on that suffix only through its conjunction with $R_D$. Hence $\mathbb E_Q(F_s\mid D)=\mu_s(D)$. Conditional Jensen gives $$\label{eq:entropy-type-conditional-rate}
 \mathbb E_D\abs{\mu_s(D)-\pi_s}\le R_t.$$

In particular, with $A=1/p_*$ and $H_{\max}=1/\omega_\top$, <a id="eq:entropy-one-control"></a>$$\label{eq:entropy-one-control}
 0\le f_D(\mathbf1)\le H_{\max},
 \qquad
 \mathbb E_D\abs{f_D(\mathbf1)-A}\le R_t/\omega_\top.$$ Indeed $\mu_\top(D)=\omega_\top f_D(\mathbf1)$ and $\pi_\top=A\omega_\top$. Since $p_*\ge\omega_\top$, we also have $H_{\max}\ge A>1$.

<a id="pm-h-35"></a>
###### Cancellation of the prefix likelihood.

<a id="eq:entropy-comparison-density"></a>
<a id="eq:entropy-first-clip-mass"></a>
For any primitive comparison $(R,R')\sim\Pi$, convexity of the positive part and the use of a shared suffix in $U_D(R)$ and $U_D(R')$ imply $$\begin{aligned}
 \mathbb E_D\mathbb E_\Pi(f_D(R)-f_D(R'))_+
 &=\frac1Z\mathbb E_{D\sim P_*^j}
       \left[\mathbf 1_{\{Z_D>0\}}
          \mathbb E_\Pi\bigl(U_D(R)-U_D(R')\bigr)_+\right]
 \notag\\
 &\le\frac1Z\mathbb E_{D,\Pi,\mathrm{suffix}}
       \mathbf 1_{\{W(R_D\wedge R\wedge R_{\mathrm{suffix}})
                  >W(R_D\wedge R'\wedge R_{\mathrm{suffix}})\}}
 \notag\\
 &\le\frac{\varepsilon v}{Z}\le u.
 \label{eq:entropy-comparison-density}
\end{aligned}$$ In the middle expectation the prefix and suffix are independent product mixture draws, independent of the coupled pair. The last bound is thus an average of Equation <a href="#eq:entropy-lifted-order" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:entropy-lifted-order">[eq:entropy-lifted-order]</a> with its one tested coordinate and $t-1$ shared context coordinates. The factor $Z_D$ from the prefix law has canceled the denominator of $f_D$. Averaging the comparisons to $\mathbf1$ over the first marginal type also gives $$\label{eq:entropy-first-clip-mass}
 \mathbb E_D\int(f_D-f_D(\mathbf1))_+\,\mathrm dP_*\le u.$$

<a id="pm-h-36"></a>
###### Clipping, including the rare-prefix entropy loss.

<a id="eq:entropy-clipping-mass"></a>
The density now satisfies the required mean and order estimates. We next retain enough of its entropy during clipping to force concentration near zero and $A$. Write $\varphi(x)=x\log x$ for $x>0$, with $\varphi(0)=0$, and set $$g_D=\min\{f_D,f_D(\mathbf1)\},
 \qquad \widehat f_D=\min\{g_D,A\}.$$ The first-moment losses are bounded separately from the entropy: $$\begin{aligned}
 \mathbb E_D\int(f_D-g_D)\,\mathrm dP_*&\le u,
 \notag\\
 \mathbb E_D\int(g_D-\widehat f_D)\,\mathrm dP_*
 &\le\mathbb E_D(f_D(\mathbf1)-A)_+\le R_t/\omega_\top.
 \label{eq:entropy-clipping-mass}
\end{aligned}$$ In particular, <a id="eq:entropy-clipped-mass"></a>$$\label{eq:entropy-clipped-mass}
 0\le1-\mathbb E_D\int\widehat f_D\,\mathrm dP_*
 \le u+R_t/\omega_\top.$$

<a id="eq:entropy-rare-prefix-loss"></a>
Take $t$ large enough that $\beta=p^2\le e^{-1}$, and consider the prefix event $\mathcal B_\beta=\{0<Z_D<\beta\}$. Since $f_D\le1/Z_D$ and $\int f_D\,\mathrm dP_*=1$, $$\int\varphi(f_D)_+\,\mathrm dP_*\le\log(1/Z_D).$$ Also $\varphi(g_D)\ge-1/e$ pointwise. Using the actual prefix density $Z_D/Z$ therefore bounds the signed entropy loss by $$\begin{aligned}
 \mathbb E_D\left[\mathbf 1_{\mathcal B_\beta}
       \int\bigl(\varphi(f_D)-\varphi(g_D)\bigr)\,\mathrm dP_*\right]
 &\le\frac1Z\mathbb E_{P_*^j}
         \left[\mathbf 1_{\mathcal B_\beta}Z_D\log(1/Z_D)\right]
          +\frac1e Q_j(\mathcal B_\beta)
 \notag\\
 &\le\frac{\beta\log(1/\beta)+\beta/e}{Z}
 \notag\\
 &\le T_t:=\frac p{c_z}\left(2tL+\frac1e\right).
 \label{eq:entropy-rare-prefix-loss}
\end{aligned}$$ Here $x\log(1/x)$ is increasing on $[0,\beta]$, $Q_j(\mathcal B_\beta)\le\beta/Z$, and the final step uses Equation <a href="#eq:entropy-normalization-lower" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:entropy-normalization-lower">[eq:entropy-normalization-lower]</a>. The term $\beta/(eZ)$ is included because the clipped entropy can be negative. Thus Equation <a href="#eq:entropy-rare-prefix-loss" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:entropy-rare-prefix-loss">[eq:entropy-rare-prefix-loss]</a> controls the signed loss, not just the positive part of the original entropy.

<a id="eq:entropy-endpoint-defect"></a>
On the complementary prefixes, $f_D\le1/\beta$. Although $\varphi'(x)=1+\log x$ is unbounded below at zero, it is bounded above there by $M_\beta=1+\log(1/\beta)=1+2tL$. Integrating this upper derivative bound, including the limit at zero, gives $$\varphi(x)-\varphi(y)\le M_\beta(x-y)
 \qquad(0\le y\le x\le1/\beta).$$ Equation <a href="#eq:entropy-first-clip-mass" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:entropy-first-clip-mass">[eq:entropy-first-clip-mass]</a> bounds the complementary expected entropy loss by $M_\beta u$. In the second clipping only values in $[A,H_{\max}]$ change; on that interval $\varphi'\le M_H:=1+\log H_{\max}$. Consequently $$\mathbb E_D\int\bigl(\varphi(g_D)-\varphi(\widehat f_D)\bigr)\,\mathrm dP_*
 \le M_H R_t/\omega_\top.$$ Combining these estimates with Equation <a href="#eq:entropy-next-lower" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:entropy-next-lower">[eq:entropy-next-lower]</a>, $$\mathbb E_D\int\varphi(\widehat f_D)\,\mathrm dP_*
 \ge L-C_I/j-T_t-M_\beta u-M_H R_t/\omega_\top.$$ Since $\log A=L$ and the mean mass of $\widehat f_D$ is at most one, we obtain the nonnegative endpoint defect bound $$\label{eq:entropy-endpoint-defect}
 0\le\mathbb E_D\int\widehat f_D\log\frac A{\widehat f_D}\,\mathrm dP_*
 \le C_I/j+T_t+M_\beta u+M_H R_t/\omega_\top.$$

<a id="pm-h-37"></a>
###### Thresholding the original density.

<a id="eq:entropy-distance"></a>
For $0\le x\le A$, $$\label{eq:entropy-distance}
 \operatorname{dist}(x,\{0,A\})\le2x\log(A/x).$$ For $x\le A/2$ this follows from $2\log(A/x)\ge2\log2>1$. For $A/2\le x\le A$, set $z=x/A$ and use $-\log z\ge1-z$ and $2z\ge1$. The case $x=0$ follows by continuity. Define a common valuation, for each admissible prefix, by thresholding the original density: $$H_D(R)=\mathbf 1_{\{f_D(R)>A/2\}}.$$ For every $x\ge0$, including $x>A$ and the tie $x=A/2$, $\abs{x-A\mathbf 1_{\{x>A/2\}}}=\operatorname{dist}(x,\{0,A\})$. Distance to a fixed set is $1$-Lipschitz: for any $x,y$ and any point $a$ of the set, $|x-a|\le|x-y|+|y-a|$, and taking the infimum proves the assertion. Applying this observation to $f_D$ and $\widehat f_D$, and using Equations <a href="#eq:entropy-clipping-mass" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:entropy-clipping-mass">[eq:entropy-clipping-mass]</a>, <a href="#eq:entropy-endpoint-defect" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:entropy-endpoint-defect">[eq:entropy-endpoint-defect]</a>, and <a href="#eq:entropy-distance" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:entropy-distance">[eq:entropy-distance]</a>, gives <a id="eq:entropy-l1-rate"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
 \rho_t
 &:=\mathbb E_D\int\abs{f_D-AH_D}\,\mathrm dP_*
 \notag\\
 &\le\frac{2C_I}{j}+2T_t+(1+2M_\beta)u
                  +(1+2M_H)\frac{R_t}{\omega_\top}
 \notag\\
 &\le C\left(t^{-1/2}+tp+(1+t)\frac{\varepsilon}{p}\right).
 \label{eq:entropy-l1-rate}
\end{aligned}$$ Here $C$ depends only on $p_*,\omega_\top,b_0,K_0,B_0$; we used $j\ge t/4$, $m\ge t/2$, and the explicit preceding definitions. This estimate has no dependence on any primitive atom. In particular, arbitrarily small $Z_D$ have been accounted for by Equation <a href="#eq:entropy-rare-prefix-loss" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:entropy-rare-prefix-loss">[eq:entropy-rare-prefix-loss]</a>, rather than excluded by an assumption of uniformly bounded conditional densities.

<a id="pm-h-38"></a>
###### One prefix for every calibration and comparison.

<a id="eq:entropy-calibration-transfer"></a>
<a id="eq:entropy-order-transfer"></a>
Let $$\rho_s(D)=\mathbb E_{P_s}\abs{f_D(R)-AH_D(R)}.$$ The positive mixture weights imply $\mathbb E_D\rho_s(D)\le\rho_t/\omega_s$. Moreover $\mu_s(D)=\omega_s\mathbb E_{P_s}f_D$ and $\pi_s=A\omega_s p_s$. The triangle inequality and Equation <a href="#eq:entropy-type-conditional-rate" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:entropy-type-conditional-rate">[eq:entropy-type-conditional-rate]</a> yield $$\label{eq:entropy-calibration-transfer}
 \mathbb E_D\abs{\mathbb E_{P_s}H_D-p_s}
 \le\frac{\rho_t+R_t}{A\omega_s}.$$ For a comparison $\Pi$ with marginals $P_s,P_{s'}$, the pointwise inequality $$A\mathbf 1_{\{H_D(R)>H_D(R')\}}
 \le\abs{AH_D(R)-f_D(R)}
      +(f_D(R)-f_D(R'))_+
      +\abs{f_D(R')-AH_D(R')}$$ and Equation <a href="#eq:entropy-comparison-density" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:entropy-comparison-density">[eq:entropy-comparison-density]</a> give $$\label{eq:entropy-order-transfer}
 \mathbb E_D\mathbb P_\Pi\bigl(H_D(R)>H_D(R')\bigr)
 \le\frac{\rho_t/\omega_s+\rho_t/\omega_{s'}+u}{A}.$$ Only the two marginal laws enter the absolute-error bounds. In particular, no density of $\Pi$ relative to a product law is needed. Likewise no $p_s$ is inverted, so these estimates include $p_s=0$.

Sum all calibration errors and comparison errors in Equation <a href="#eq:entropy-valuation-target" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:entropy-valuation-target">[eq:entropy-valuation-target]</a> to form a nonnegative quantity $\Theta(D)$. Equations <a href="#eq:entropy-calibration-transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:entropy-calibration-transfer">[eq:entropy-calibration-transfer]</a> and <a href="#eq:entropy-order-transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:entropy-order-transfer">[eq:entropy-order-transfer]</a> show that $$\mathbb E_D\Theta(D)
 \le\sum_{s\in\mathcal T}\frac{\rho_t+R_t}{A\omega_s}
 +\sum_{\Pi\in\mathcal C}
   \frac{\rho_t/\omega_{s(\Pi)}
          +\rho_t/\omega_{s'(\Pi)}+u}{A}.$$ The sums have fixed finite length. Since $0<p_*<1$, $t^{-1/2}+tp_*^t$ tends to zero. First choose $t_0$ large enough that these terms make the displayed bound less than $\zeta/2$ for every $t\ge t_0$, and also that $p_*^{2t}\le e^{-1}$. For each such $t$, choose $\varepsilon_0(t)>0$ small enough to satisfy Equation <a href="#eq:entropy-epsilon-small" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:entropy-epsilon-small">[eq:entropy-epsilon-small]</a> and to make all remaining $\varepsilon/p$ terms contribute less than $\zeta/2$. Shrinking $\varepsilon_0(t)$ if necessary makes the final bound strictly less than $\zeta$. Some positive-$Q_j$ prefix then has $\Theta(D)<\zeta$. Every individual required error is nonnegative, so the single valuation $H=H_D$ satisfies all of Equation <a href="#eq:entropy-valuation-target" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:entropy-valuation-target">[eq:entropy-valuation-target]</a>. Its dependence only on actual predicates follows from Equation <a href="#eq:entropy-common-density" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:entropy-common-density">[eq:entropy-common-density]</a>. All choices used only the fixed numerical data stated in the lemma, completing the proof. ◻

</details>

For the primitive system of Section <a href="#sec:events" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:events">4</a>, choose the tolerance from Lemma <a href="#lem:event-obstruction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:event-obstruction">11</a>. Lemma <a href="#lem:rare-event" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:rare-event">12</a> then says that, on a NO instance, no common conjunction response can satisfy Equations <a href="#eq:entropy-iid-success" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:entropy-iid-success">[eq:entropy-iid-success]</a>–<a href="#eq:entropy-lifted-order" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:entropy-lifted-order">[eq:entropy-lifted-order]</a> for those choices of $t$ and $\varepsilon$. The constants $b_0,K_0,B_0$ must be fixed before this choice; the next two sections produce precisely that interface from a putative sparse cut.

<a id="sec:scores"></a>
### Scores, comparison measures, and an amplitude signal

A *score* is a real function on the cube of assignments to the bit positions of the test system. All scores below depend on a bounded number of positions. Identical functions are identified, even if their sampling presentations differ. A lookup $h$ is a measurable function from scores to $\{0,1\}$; measurability means Borel measurability on each finite table of score values. A raw comparison form is a finite positive measure on pairs of scores, evaluated as $$\mathcal D(h)=\int |h(B)-h(B')|\,\mathrm d\mathfrak m(B,B').$$ Its truth cost at an assignment $x$ is $\int|B(x)-B'(x)|\,\mathrm d\mathfrak m(B,B')$.

<a id="stmt-thm-score-gap"></a>
<div id="thm:score-gap" class="theorem">

**Theorem 13** (Analytic score gap). *For each fixed $T_0\ge1$, the primitive system of Section <a href="#sec:events" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:events">4</a> admits a demand law $\mu$ of scores and a finite sum $\mathcal D$ of raw comparison forms with the following properties. All arities, score bounds, numerical dimensions, and total comparison weight are bounded by constants independent of the input size. The laws and weights do not depend on a lookup. At a perfect witness $x$, $$\int|B(x)-B'(x)|\,\mathrm d\mathfrak m(B,B')\le4,
 \qquad
 \mu\{B:B(x)\ge1/4\},\ \mu\{B:B(x)\le-1/4\}\ge10^{-4}.$$ In a NO instance, every binary measurable lookup with $\nu=\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}_\mu(h)>0$ satisfies $\mathcal D(h)>T_0\nu$. Conditional on the discrete sampling data, the numerical laws and score entries belong to fixed finite families of polynomials with rational coefficients on rational boxes, with atoms represented as separate cases.*

</div>

We first construct the demand law and comparison measures, with bounded truth cost at a satisfying assignment. For a putative low-cost coloring, the resampling comparison supplies an amplitude signal. The remaining comparisons preserve enough of this signal to obtain the common conjunction response required by Lemma <a href="#lem:rare-event" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:rare-event">12</a>. Section <a href="#sec:paths" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:paths">7</a> constructs that response and completes the proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:score-gap" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:score-gap">13</a>.

It suffices to prove the theorem for rational $T_0\ge1$, since one can replace a given real $T_0$ by a larger rational number. Choose positive rational mixture weights $\omega_s$ for all primitive types, and write $P_*$ for their mixture and $p_*=\sum_s\omega_sp_s\in(0,1)$. A length-$t$ conjunction from the independent mixture law has honest probability $p=p_*^t$. A fixed pattern of primitive types has honest probability equal to the product of their $p_s$’s. The ordered conjunction couplings change one coordinate by one of the prescribed primitive couplings and use independent, shared, fixed-type samples in all other coordinates. They give actual pointwise inclusions $r\le r'$.

<a id="pm-h-40"></a>
#### Demand and random half-freezing

<a id="eq:score-scales"></a>
Let $M$ be a positive integer. Stage $j$ contains $n_j$ slots, where $n_j$ is even. Choose a positive rational coefficient satisfying $$\label{eq:score-scales}
 \frac1{\sqrt{pMn_j}}\le s_j\le\frac2{\sqrt{pMn_j}},\qquad s_j\le1.$$ All stage sizes will be chosen below. In each slot independently sample a primary presentation $\mathcal R_i$ of an iid conjunction $r_i$ and an independent amplitude with law $$\lambda=\tfrac12\delta_0+\tfrac12\operatorname{Unif}[-1,1].$$ The demand score and its law are $$B^0=\sum_{j=1}^M s_j\sum_{i\text{ in stage }j}a_i r_i,
 \qquad \mu=\operatorname{Law}(B^0).$$ At a perfect witness $x$, each summand is symmetric, centered, and bounded by one, with variance $s_j^2p/6$. Consequently $$\frac16\le\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(B^0(x))\le\frac23,\qquad
 \mathbb EB^0(x)^4
 \le\sum_{j,i}\mathbb E(s_ja_ir_i(x))^2+3\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(B^0(x))^2<4.$$ For $X=B^0(x)$, Cauchy–Schwarz gives $$\mathbb P(|X|\ge1/4)
 \ge\frac{(\mathbb EX^2-1/16)^2}{\mathbb EX^4}
 \ge\frac{25}{9216}.$$ Symmetry splits this probability equally between the two required tails.

<a id="eq:assumed-score-cut"></a>
<a id="eq:resampling-bound"></a>
Set $\rho=1/(2048T_0^2)$. Include the form with weight $16T_0$ that compares $B^0$ with $B^{0,\rho}$, obtained by independently resampling each complete primary slot with probability $\rho$. Its truth cost is at most $$16T_0\sqrt{2\rho\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(B^0(x))}
 \le16T_0\sqrt{4\rho/3}=1/\sqrt6<1.$$ Throughout the soundness proof suppose, for a contradiction, that $$\label{eq:assumed-score-cut}
 0<\nu=\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(h(B^0)),\qquad \mathcal D(h)\le T_0\nu.$$ In particular, $$\label{eq:resampling-bound}
 \mathbb E|h(B^0)-h(B^{0,\rho})|\le\nu/16.$$

Independently choose a uniform half of the slots in each stage to freeze. Let $F_j$ be that half, $I_j$ its complement, and $F=\bigcup_jF_j$. The information $\mathcal Z$ consists of the freeze sets and all complete primary variables in frozen slots. Let $\mathcal B$ add to $\mathcal Z$ the primary presentations of all unfrozen slots, but none of their amplitudes. Define, for $i\in I_j$, $$s_jb_i^0=\mathbb E[h(B^0)\mid\mathcal B,a_i]-\mathbb E[h(B^0)\mid\mathcal B],
 \qquad g_i^0=\mathbb E[b_i^0\mid\mathcal Z,\mathcal R_i,a_i].$$ Indices in such formulas are fixed after the freeze sets are specified. For any family $b=(b_i)$ put $$\|b\|_\Sigma^2=\mathbb E\sum_j s_j^2\sum_{i\in I_j}|b_i|^2.$$

<a id="stmt-lem-amplitude-signal"></a>
<div id="lem:amplitude-signal" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:amplitude-signal"></a>
**Lemma 14** (Amplitude signal). *Choose $k=\lceil\rho^{-1}\rceil$ and require $n_j\ge4k$. With $c=4^{-(k+2)}$, Equation <a href="#eq:resampling-bound" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:resampling-bound">[eq:resampling-bound]</a> implies $$\label{eq:amplitude-signal}
 \|b^0\|_\Sigma^2\le\nu,\qquad
 \sum_j s_j^2\sum_{i\in I_j}\mathbb E[|b_i^0|^2\mid\mathcal B]\le\tfrac14,
 \qquad \|g^0\|_\Sigma^2\ge c\nu.$$ The constants $k,c$ depend only on $T_0$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-amplitude-signal"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-amplitude-signal</summary>

*Proof.* First consider a single slot. Its erasure observation $O$ is a distinguished symbol $*$ when $a=0$, and $(\mathcal R,a)$ otherwise. For a square-integrable function $\psi(O)$, write $c_*=\psi(*)$, let $\psi_1(\mathcal R,u)$ be its nonerased value, and put $m=\mathbb E\psi_1$. Then $$\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(\mathbb E[\psi(O)\mid\mathcal R])
 =\tfrac14\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(\mathbb E_u\psi_1)\le\tfrac14\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(\psi_1),
 \quad
 \mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(\psi(O))=\tfrac12\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(\psi_1)+\tfrac14(c_*-m)^2.$$ Thus the conditional expectation from centered functions of $O$ to functions of $\mathcal R$ has norm at most $2^{-1/2}$.

<a id="eq:erasure-contraction"></a>
The centered function $f=h(B^0)-\mathbb Eh(B^0)$ is a function of the independent erasure observations: when a slot has amplitude zero its primary presentation contributes nothing to the score. Decompose $f$ by active sets of observation coordinates, using the classical orthogonal product-space decomposition of Hoeffding and Efron–Stein (Hoeffding 1948; Efron and Stein 1981) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[Hoeffding1948](#ref-Hoeffding1948), [EfronStein1981](#ref-EfronStein1981)]. If $P_i$ averages observation $i$, the component on $S\ne\varnothing$ is $f_S=\prod_{i\in S}(I-P_i)\prod_{i\notin S}P_if$. These components are orthogonal and separately centered in their active coordinates. Conditional expectation onto all primary presentations preserves this separate centering, so the images of different active sets remain orthogonal. Applying the one-slot bound in each active coordinate gives $$\label{eq:erasure-contraction}
 \|\mathbb E[f\mid(\mathcal R_i)_i]\|_2^2
 \le\sum_{S\ne\varnothing}2^{-|S|}\|f_S\|_2^2\le\nu/2.$$

<a id="eq:low-degree-amplitude"></a>
Refine the decomposition on complete primary slots. Let $\mathsf P_i$ average the whole slot and $\mathsf R_i$ average its amplitude alone. The operators $$\mathsf C_i=\mathsf P_i,\qquad
 \mathsf D_i=\mathsf R_i-\mathsf P_i,\qquad
 \mathsf A_i=I-\mathsf R_i$$ are orthogonal projections onto, respectively, constants, centered functions of primary data, and functions with conditional amplitude mean zero. Operators in distinct slots commute. Write $f_\eta=\prod_i\eta_if$ for $\eta_i\in\{\mathsf C_i,\mathsf D_i,\mathsf A_i\}$, and let $S(\eta)=\{i:\eta_i\ne\mathsf C_i\}$ and $A(\eta)=\{i:\eta_i=\mathsf A_i\}$. Equation <a href="#eq:erasure-contraction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:erasure-contraction">[eq:erasure-contraction]</a> says that the total squared mass with $A(\eta)\ne\varnothing$ is at least $\nu/2$. Slot resampling acts on $f_\eta$ by $(1-\rho)^{|S(\eta)|}$; hence $$\mathbb E(h(B^0)-h(B^{0,\rho}))^2
 =2\sum_\eta[1-(1-\rho)^{|S(\eta)|}]\|f_\eta\|_2^2.$$ Binary differences have equal absolute and squared values. For $|S(\eta)|>k$, the bracketed coefficient multiplied by two is at least one. Equation <a href="#eq:resampling-bound" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:resampling-bound">[eq:resampling-bound]</a> bounds the mass of these components by $\nu/16$. In particular, $$\label{eq:low-degree-amplitude}
 \sum_{A(\eta)\ne\varnothing,\ |S(\eta)|\le k}
       \|f_\eta\|_2^2\ge3\nu/8.$$

Given $\mathcal B$, the unfrozen amplitudes are independent. The variables $s_jb_i^0$ are the orthogonal projections of $h(B^0)-\mathbb E[h(B^0)\mid\mathcal B]$ onto their separate centered amplitude spaces. Bessel’s inequality bounds their conditional squared norms by $\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(h(B^0)\mid\mathcal B)\le1/4$. Averaging and using total variance proves the first two assertions.

The tower property and amplitude independence give exactly $$s_jg_i^0=\mathbb E[h(B^0)\mid\mathcal Z,\mathcal R_i,a_i]
             -\mathbb E[h(B^0)\mid\mathcal Z,\mathcal R_i].$$ For a fixed freeze set, this is the projection $\mathsf A_i\prod_{\ell\notin F\cup\{i\}}\mathsf P_\ell f$. After averaging the observed frozen values as well, orthogonality yields $$\|g^0\|_\Sigma^2
 =\sum_\eta\|f_\eta\|_2^2
   \sum_{i\in A(\eta)}
   \mathbb P_F(i\notin F,\ S(\eta)\setminus\{i\}\subseteq F).$$ For one component in Equation <a href="#eq:low-degree-amplitude" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:low-degree-amplitude">[eq:low-degree-amplitude]</a>, distinguish an $i\in A(\eta)$ in stage $j$, put $d=|S(\eta)|\le k$, and let $q_\ell$ count its active slots in stage $\ell$. With falling factorials, the displayed capture probability is $$\frac12\frac{(n_j/2)_{q_j-1}}{(n_j-1)_{q_j-1}}
       \prod_{\ell\ne j}\frac{(n_\ell/2)_{q_\ell}}{(n_\ell)_{q_\ell}}.$$ Each of its $d-1$ ratio factors is at least $1/4$: its numerator is at least $n_\ell/2-k+1\ge n_\ell/4$ and denominator at most $n_\ell$. The probability is therefore at least $\tfrac12 4^{-(d-1)}\ge4^{-k}$. Keeping just the distinguished contribution and using Equation <a href="#eq:low-degree-amplitude" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:low-degree-amplitude">[eq:low-degree-amplitude]</a> gives the claimed $c\nu$. ◻

</details>

We must turn this amplitude signal into the response on actual conjunction predicates required by Lemma <a href="#lem:rare-event" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:rare-event">12</a>. We will first construct a real response, bound its size under each type pattern, and control its decrease when $r$ is replaced by a containing predicate $r'$. Three kinds of comparison path provide these estimates. Paths between opposite contributions of one conjunction bound the response under each pattern; paths using $r\le r'$ control its order; paths through the random-amplitude contribution carry the signal to that response. The comparison measures sample their path orientations independently of the lookup. A common orientation extracted from the lookup will enter only the analysis. The estimates must retain errors proportional to $\nu$ throughout, so that thresholding the response can meet the rare-event criterion even when the cut variance is arbitrarily small.

<a id="pm-h-41"></a>
#### The order of the constants

<a id="eq:preconjunction-constants"></a>
<a id="eq:delta-choice"></a>
<a id="eq:stage-number"></a>
We first fix the signal-loss tolerance $e$, the clipping bound $B_*$, and the signal threshold $\gamma$, together with the resulting inputs $b_0,K_0,B_0$ to Lemma <a href="#lem:rare-event" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:rare-event">12</a>. Choose rational constants $$\label{eq:preconjunction-constants}
 \begin{gathered}
 c_1=c/4,\quad 0<e\le c/160000,\quad
 B_*\ge\max(1,3200(T_0+1)/c),\quad K_*=32(T_0+2),\\
 0<\gamma\le c_1/(16K_*),\quad
 b_*=c_1/(128B_*^2),\quad
 b_0=b_*/[8(T_0+1)],\quad K_0=16/\gamma,\quad B_0=B_*.
 \end{gathered}$$ These constants have no dependence on conjunction length. Apply Lemma <a href="#lem:rare-event" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:rare-event">12</a> to $b_0,K_0,B_0$ and choose its length $t$ and tolerance $\varepsilon>0$. We can decrease $\varepsilon$ to make it rational. From now on $p=p_*^t$ is fixed. Let $V$ be the number of fixed type patterns together with the iid conjunction law, and let $Q$ be the number of required ordered conjunction couplings, counting the changed coordinate. Our path menu below has $L=V+2Q+2$ entries. Set $A_t=16V+80Q$, and choose rational $\Delta>0$ so small that $$\label{eq:delta-choice}
 \Delta\le b_*p\gamma/160,\qquad
 \left(\frac{16p}{\gamma\varepsilon}+1\right)
          \frac{A_t\Delta}{\gamma}\le b_*p/4.$$ Choose an integer $$\label{eq:stage-number}
 M\ge\frac{32B_*K_*(T_0+3)}{c_1\Delta}.$$ The remaining parameters are chosen sequentially in Subsection <a href="#subsec:funding" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="subsec:funding">6.5</a>. This order will be justified by estimates whose constants are independent of the later stage sizes.

<a id="pm-h-42"></a>
#### Paths, replacements, and formal cells

Start with the one-cell partition $C_0$ of the assignment cube. At stage $j$, given the preceding partition $C_{j-1}$, use these path laws $D^-\longrightarrow D^+$ for a sign function $\tau$ constant on its cells:

1.  $-\tau r\longrightarrow+\tau r$ for each of the $V$ vertical laws;

2.  $+\tau r\longrightarrow+\tau r'$ and $-\tau r'\longrightarrow-\tau r$ for each ordered pair $r\le r'$;

3.  $-\tau r\longrightarrow ar$ and $ar\longrightarrow+\tau r$, where $r$ is iid and $a\sim\lambda$ is fresh.

Every endpoint has absolute value at most one, and every coordinate of $\tau(D^+-D^-)$ is nonnegative. All predicates and any amplitude in a path are fresh samples from its specified law.

Independently in each unfrozen slot of stage $j$, retain the primary summand with probability $1-\alpha_j$; call this choice shared. Otherwise draw a uniform menu entry, its fresh path data, a uniform descriptor $\tau\in\{-1,+1\}^{J_{j-1}}$, a uniform time $u\in[0,1]$, and a fair sign $\chi\in\{-1,+1\}$. Replace that slot by $$s_j\chi[(1-u)D^-+uD^+].$$ Here $J_{j-1}$ is the number of formal cells at the preceding level. The score after all replacements through stage $j$ is $B^j$; future slots and frozen slots still have their primary summands.

Construct $C_j$ by refining $C_{j-1}$ with all queried positions in the current unfrozen primary presentations, including those in replaced slots, and all queried positions in the current fresh paths. Use fixed orders and pad each list to a deterministic length with coordinates whose value is identically zero. Retain all formal binary cells, including inconsistent and empty ones. Thus $J_j$ is a deterministic constant once the sizes through stage $j$ are fixed. A descriptor defines the indicated sign function on actual cells; values on empty cells do not matter. This construction inspects neither amplitudes, times, nor signs when forming the partition after its question lists are known. Frozen and future query lists do not enter $C_j$.

The whole experiment first draws primary samples and freeze sets, then performs these independent stage trials given the preceding partitions. Trials use no lookup and no analytic reference signs. Although a reference sign defined below can depend on future primary summands, it never enters the generative history.

At truth, conditional on frozen variables and earlier actual trials, the current summand differences are independent and centered. Their second moments are at most $4s_j^2\alpha_j$. Thus <a id="eq:replacement-truth"></a>$$\label{eq:replacement-truth}
 \mathbb E|B^j(x)-B^{j-1}(x)|
 \le\delta_j:=4\sqrt{\alpha_j/(pM)}.$$ For each $j$, let $S_j$ be the first $m_j$ indices of $I_j$ in their fixed order. Delete their current summands from $B^j$ to obtain $B_\circ^j$, and delete only slot $i$ to obtain $B^j_{\neg i}$. Conditional on the other summands and preceding history, these deleted bases do not use the variables in the deleted slots. The single-slot deletion will provide the background for varying that slot’s predicate. Deleting all of $S_j$ will let us choose one orientation, from the remaining score and preceding partitions, for every tested slot.

<a id="pm-h-43"></a>
#### Smoothing and the comparison measures

<a id="eq:density-identities"></a>
<a id="eq:reference-sign"></a>
For $\xi>0$ use the probability density $$f_\xi(a)=\frac{15}{16\xi}(1-a^2/\xi^2)^2\mathbf 1_{[-\xi,\xi]}(a).$$ It is even, continuously differentiable after extension by zero, and decreasing on $[0,\xi]$. Direct integration gives $$\label{eq:density-identities}
 \int|f_\xi'|=\frac{15}{8\xi},\qquad
 \int_0^\xi 2b[-f_\xi'(b)]\,\mathrm db=1.$$ For every $z\in C_\ell$, let $\theta_{\ell,z}$ have this density with scale $\xi_\ell$, independently. Put $\Theta_\ell=\sum_{z\in C_\ell}\theta_{\ell,z}\mathbf 1_z$ and $\Theta_{\le j}=\sum_{\ell=0}^j\Theta_\ell$. With partitions fixed, set $$h_j(B)=\mathbb E_\theta h(B+\Theta_{\le j}),\qquad
 K_{j,z}(B)=\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial a}
                   h_j(B+a\mathbf 1_z)\right|_{a=0}.$$ Translation of the last density proves continuous differentiability in all cell shifts, even for merely measurable $h$. Indeed the translated density is continuously differentiable in $L^1$, and $h$ is bounded. Derivatives on empty cells vanish. Write $K_j=K_j(B^j)$ and $|K_j|_1=\sum_z|K_{j,z}|$. The map $\pi$ sums fine coordinates on each parent cell. Define the common reference descriptor $$\label{eq:reference-sign}
 \sigma^j=\operatorname{sign}K_{j-1}(B_\circ^j),$$ with ties positive and using only partitions through level $j-1$. It is common to every tested index in $S_j$.

For each mode and formal descriptor, a match in a slot means replacement with that mode and descriptor and $\chi=+1$. Its probability is $$\beta_j=\frac{\alpha_j}{2L2^{J_{j-1}}}.$$ Mark a consistent cell in $C_j$ if any of these conditions holds:

1.  For some mode and descriptor, the match fraction in $S_j$ exceeds $2\beta_j$.

2.  For some vertical mode and descriptor, the fraction in $S_j$ of matches whose path predicate is one at the cell exceeds $2\beta_jp_{\rm law}$.

3.  The fraction in $I_j$ that is shared and has primary predicate one at the cell exceeds $2p$.

<a id="eq:marking-truth"></a>
The first condition, if it occurs, marks every consistent cell. All these rules use actual query values and trial data, and do not use $h$. Every descriptor is checked, so on an unmarked cell the empirical bounds also hold for the lookup-dependent descriptor $\sigma^j$. They will bound match-weighted gradient sums without assuming that the fine gradient is independent of the match indicators. A separate comparison form below will charge for gradient mass on the marked cells. At a fixed perfect witness, condition on frozen data and earlier actual trials. Indicators in each count are independent over current slots. Plain matches have mean $\beta_j$, vertical hit matches have mean $\beta_jp_{\rm law}$, and shared hits have mean at most $p$. For $m$ independent indicators of mean $b>0$, Chebyshev’s inequality bounds the probability that their average exceeds $2b$ by $1/(mb)$. A zero mean gives a zero count almost surely. If $p_{\min}$ is the minimum positive vertical honest probability (including $p$), a union bound gives $$\label{eq:marking-truth}
 \mathbb P(x\text{ lies in a marked cell})
 \le\frac{2L2^{J_{j-1}}}{m_j\beta_jp_{\min}}+\frac2{n_jp}.$$

We now specify every additional raw form. All expectations below include the indicated score experiment; numerical noises are shared whenever their levels appear in both arguments.

For any set $A$ of level-$j$ cells, define $$N_j(B;A)=\mathbb E\sum_{z\in A}\int_0^{\xi_j}[-f_{\xi_j}'(b)]
 \big|h(B+\Theta_{\le j}^{z,+b})
           -h(B+\Theta_{\le j}^{z,-b})\big|\,\mathrm db,$$ where the superscript fixes only coordinate $(j,z)$ and all other noises are averaged. Translating the density and pairing $b$ with $-b$ gives $$K_{j,z}(B)=\int_0^{\xi_j}[-f_{\xi_j}'(b)]
 \mathbb E_{\rm other\ noises}
 [h(B+\Theta_{\le j}^{z,+b})-h(B+\Theta_{\le j}^{z,-b})]\,\mathrm db.$$ Thus $N_j$ dominates the expected variation on its tested cells. Its truth cost is at most the probability that the witness cell is in $A$: all other coordinates contribute zero, and the contributing integral is one by Equation <a href="#eq:density-identities" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:density-identities">[eq:density-identities]</a>. Include $N_M(B^M;C_M)$ with weight one and $N_j(B^j;\text{marked cells})$ with weight $T_0/a_j$, where $a_j=\min(p,\Delta\beta_j)$.

For $A=B^{j-1}$ or $A=B_\circ^j$, include with weight $T_0M$ the form $$P_j(A)=\mathbb E\sum_{z\in C_{j-1}}
 \int_{-\xi_{j-1}}^{\xi_{j-1}}|f_{\xi_{j-1}}'(b)|
 \big|h(B^j+\Theta_{<j}^{z,b}+\Theta_j)
              -h(A+\Theta_{<j}^{z,b})\big|\,\mathrm db.$$ Here the fixed coordinate is $(j-1,z)$ and all other preceding noises have their usual law. Differentiating with respect to this preceding coordinate shifts all its children equally in the first term; its derivative is $(\pi K_j)_z$. The second derivative is $K_{j-1,z}(A)$. Taking absolute values under the common integral proves $$\mathbb E|\pi K_j-K_{j-1}(A)|_1\le P_j(A).$$ With $\Lambda_{j-1}=15J_{j-1}/(8\xi_{j-1})$, its unweighted truth cost is at most $\Lambda_{j-1}(\delta_j+\xi_j)$ for the first choice of $A$, and $\Lambda_{j-1}(m_js_j+\xi_j)$ for the second.

Include also the following ordinary coupled raw differences:

1.  Compare $B^0$ to $B^j+\Theta_{<j}$, with weight $T_0M/e$.

2.  For uniform $i\in I_j$ forced shared, compare $B^j+\Theta_{\le j}$ to $B^j+\Theta_{<j}$, with weight $T_0/(e\,p\,s_j^2)$.

3.  For each mode and each of its two endpoints, force uniform $i\in S_j$ into that mode with independent uniform descriptor and $\chi=+1$. Rebuild $C_j$ using its fresh path data. For $B=B^j_{\neg i}+s_jD^\pm$, compare $B+\Theta_{\le j}$ to $B+\Theta_{<j}$, with weight $T_0/(s_j\Delta2^{-J_{j-1}})$.

The forced trial has its prescribed conditional law given earlier stages, and all other trials retain their ordinary laws. The primary presentation remains in the query list. The two last types of form have unweighted truth cost at most $\xi_j$. The first has cost at most $\sum_{k\le j}\delta_k+\sum_{\ell<j}\xi_\ell$.

All coefficients in these measures are nonnegative. Their laws use uniform descriptors; the lookup-dependent descriptor $\sigma^j$ in Equation <a href="#eq:reference-sign" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:reference-sign">[eq:reference-sign]</a> occurs only in the analysis.

<a id="subsec:funding"></a>
#### A sequential rational funding schedule

The schedule makes all comparison truth costs simultaneously small while retaining the variance-relative estimates needed below. Every choice uses only parameters already fixed.

Put $\eta=1/[100M(L+1)]$ and $d_{\rm cap}=\eta e/(4T_0M^2)$. Choose positive rational $\xi_0\le d_{\rm cap}$. At stage $j$, the numbers $J=J_{j-1}$ and $\Lambda=\Lambda_{j-1}$ are already known. Choose the following parameters in this order:

1.  Choose rational $0<\alpha_j\le\min(1/2,e)$ so small that $$\delta_j\le\min(d_{\rm cap},\eta/(2T_0M\Lambda)).$$ Squaring the right-hand bound supplies a rational sufficient condition. Set $\beta_j=\alpha_j/(2L2^J)$ and $a_j=\min(p,\Delta\beta_j)$.

2.  Choose an integer $$m_j\ge\frac{2T_0(2L2^J)}{\eta a_j\beta_jp_{\min}}.$$

3.  Choose an even integer $$n_j\ge\max\left(2m_j,4k,\frac4{pM},\frac{4T_0}{\eta a_jp},
                 \frac{16T_0^2M\Lambda^2m_j^2}{\eta^2p}\right),$$ and rational $s_j$ satisfying Equation <a href="#eq:score-scales" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:score-scales">[eq:score-scales]</a>. Such a rational can be found between the two endpoints by rational interval refinement; the bound $n_j\ge4/(pM)$ ensures $s_j\le1$.

4.  Choose rational $\xi_j>0$ with $$\xi_j\le\min\left(d_{\rm cap},\frac\eta{2T_0M\Lambda},
              \frac{\eta e p s_j^2}{T_0},
              \frac{\eta s_j\Delta2^{-J}}{T_0}\right).$$

The marked form costs at most $\eta$ by Equation <a href="#eq:marking-truth" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:marking-truth">[eq:marking-truth]</a>: its two terms cost at most $\eta/2$ each after multiplication by $T_0/a_j$. Each projected-gradient form costs at most $\eta$; for deletion use $m_js_j\le\eta/(2T_0M\Lambda)$, which follows from the last lower bound on $n_j$. Each forced form costs at most $\eta$. The cumulative demand comparison costs at most $(T_0M/e)2Md_{\rm cap}=\eta/2$. There are $5+2L$ tunable forms per stage, so their total truth cost is less than $M(5+2L)\eta<1$. Together with resampling and final variation this is less than three, and in particular at most four. All earlier truth-cost estimates are uniform over the later primary backgrounds, so subsequent parameter choices preserve the allocated bounds.

<a id="eq:funded-bounds"></a>
<a id="eq:forced-shared-bound"></a>
<a id="eq:forced-endpoint-bound"></a>
The consequences of Equation <a href="#eq:assumed-score-cut" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:assumed-score-cut">[eq:assumed-score-cut]</a> are $$\label{eq:funded-bounds}
 \begin{gathered}
 \mathbb E|K_M|_1\le T_0\nu,\qquad
 \mathbb E\sum_{z\text{ marked}}|K_{j,z}|\le a_j\nu,\\
 \mathbb E|\pi K_j-K_{j-1}|_1\le\nu/M,\qquad
 \mathbb E|\pi K_j-K_{j-1}(B_\circ^j)|_1\le\nu/M,\\
 \mathbb E|h(B^0)-h_{j-1}(B^j)|^2\le e\nu/M.
 \end{gathered}$$ The square bound follows by averaging raw comparisons and using $|u-v|^2\le|u-v|$ for $u,v\in[0,1]$. In addition, $$\label{eq:forced-shared-bound}
 \mathbb E^{i\ {\rm uniform\ shared}}
      |h_j(B^j)-h_{j-1}(B^j)|\le e p s_j^2\nu,$$ and each uniform-descriptor endpoint experiment satisfies $$\label{eq:forced-endpoint-bound}
 \mathbb E|h_j(B)-h_{j-1}(B)|\le s_j\Delta2^{-J_{j-1}}\nu.$$

<a id="pm-h-45"></a>
#### Orientation and clipping

The comparison measures and all numerical parameters are now fixed. It remains to locate a stage at which a common amplitude signal survives the accumulated approximation and clipping errors.

<a id="eq:U-mean"></a>
Put $$U=\max_{0\le j\le M}\mathbb E[|K_j|_1\mid\mathcal B],\qquad
 v=\mathbb E[U\mid\mathcal Z].$$ For each $j$, let $E_j=\mathbb E[|\pi K_j-K_{j-1}|_1\mid\mathcal B]$. The pointwise inequality $|K_{j-1}|_1\le|K_j|_1+
|\pi K_j-K_{j-1}|_1$ gives $U\le\mathbb E[|K_M|_1\mid\mathcal B]+\sum_jE_j$. Therefore $$\label{eq:U-mean}
 \mathbb EU=\mathbb Ev\le(T_0+1)\nu.$$ Define $d_{j,z}=|K_{j,z}|-\sigma^j_{\operatorname{par}(z)}K_{j,z}\ge0$. For vectors $a,b$ and $\sigma=\operatorname{sign}b$, $|a|_1-\sigma\cdot a\le2|a-b|_1$. Applying this with $a=\pi K_j$, $b=K_{j-1}(B_\circ^j)$, and then the across-stage comparison, gives $$\sum_zd_{j,z}\le |K_j|_1-|K_{j-1}|_1
       +|\pi K_j-K_{j-1}|_1
       +2|\pi K_j-K_{j-1}(B_\circ^j)|_1.$$ Summing and dropping $-|K_0|_1$ proves <a id="eq:defect-telescope"></a>$$\label{eq:defect-telescope}
 \sum_j\mathbb E\sum_zd_{j,z}\le(T_0+3)\nu.$$ Call $j$ good when its expected defect is at most $\Delta\nu$.

Transferring the amplitude signal requires both smoothing levels. A current predicate $r_i$ can split a cell of $C_{j-1}$, so smoothing along the preceding cells need not permit differentiation in its direction. Fine smoothing $h_j$ does: $r_i$ is constant on every cell of $C_j$. Its smoothing law, however, retains the current query partition and can depend on the predicate’s presentation. The coarse smoothing $h_{j-1}$ omits that partition, while its score still contains the contribution of $r_i$. Section <a href="#sec:paths" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:paths">7</a> will use this omission to express the coarse amplitude projection through a common deleted-slot background, depending on the current presentation only through the actual predicate. We therefore preserve the coarse component as the source of the response and use the fine component for derivative estimates.

<a id="eq:component-approximations"></a>
For $i\in I_j$, define $b_i$ as the centered amplitude component of $h_{j-1}(B^j)$ divided by $s_j$ under the experiment forcing $i$ shared: $$s_jb_i=\mathbb E^{i,\rm sh}[h_{j-1}(B^j)\mid\mathcal B,a_i]
                 -\mathbb E^{i,\rm sh}[h_{j-1}(B^j)\mid\mathcal B].$$ Define $b_i^+$ by replacing $h_{j-1}$ with $h_j$, and put $g_i=\mathbb E[b_i\mid\mathcal Z,\mathcal R_i,a_i]$. Forcing shared does not alter the primary or freeze law. On replacement the score and partition ignore the primary amplitude $a_i$. Thus the corresponding amplitude projection under the ordinary law is exactly $(1-\alpha_j)s_jb_i$. Conditional Bessel inequalities, applied at each stage to the last line of Equation <a href="#eq:funded-bounds" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:funded-bounds">[eq:funded-bounds]</a>, give $$\|((1-\alpha_j)b_i-b_i^0)_i\|_\Sigma^2\le e\nu.$$ Since $\alpha_j\le e\le1/2$ and $\|b^0\|_\Sigma\le\sqrt\nu$, solving for $b-b^0$ gives $$\label{eq:component-approximations}
 \|b-b^0\|_\Sigma\le4\sqrt{e\nu},\qquad
 \|b^+-b\|_\Sigma\le\sqrt{2e\nu}.$$ For the second bound, projection contraction in each forced experiment bounds $s_j^2\mathbb E|b_i^+-b_i|^2$ by the expected squared difference of the two smoothed values. Sum over $i$, use Equation <a href="#eq:forced-shared-bound" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:forced-shared-bound">[eq:forced-shared-bound]</a>, and use $|I_j|s_j^2\le2/(pM)$.

<a id="eq:component-gradient"></a>
<a id="eq:component-L1"></a>
Fine differentiation along the primary amplitude gives, for every $a$, $$\label{eq:component-gradient}
 |b_i^+(a)|\le\frac4{1-\alpha_j}
 \mathbb E\left[\mathbf 1_{\{i\ {\rm shared}\}}
             \sum_z|K_{j,z}|r_i(z)\,\middle|\,\mathcal B\right]\le8U.$$ To check the first inequality, bound the centered amplitude value by the integral of absolute derivatives over the whole interval $[-1,1]$. The derivative of $h_j$ along $a$ is $s_j\sum_zK_{j,z}r_i(z)$. The partition is fixed along this amplitude path. Lebesgue measure on $[-1,1]$ is bounded by $4\lambda$, giving the factor four; conditioning a trial to be shared gives $1/(1-\alpha_j)$. Average Equation <a href="#eq:component-gradient" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:component-gradient">[eq:component-gradient]</a> over $I_j$. On unmarked cells the shared-hit fraction is at most $2p$, and on marked cells at most one. Equations <a href="#eq:funded-bounds" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:funded-bounds">[eq:funded-bounds]</a> and <a href="#eq:U-mean" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:U-mean">[eq:U-mean]</a> imply $$\label{eq:component-L1}
 \mathbb E\operatorname{avg}_{i\in I_j}|b_i^+|
 \le16p(T_0+1)\nu+8p\nu\le K_*p\nu.$$

We now clip the fine component to obtain a bounded signal. Set $A=\{U\le B_*,\ v\le B_*\}$, which is $\mathcal B$-measurable, and define $\widetilde b_i=b_i^+\mathbf 1_A$ and $\widetilde g_i=\mathbb E[\widetilde b_i\mid\mathcal Z,\mathcal R_i,a_i]$.

<div id="tab:amplitude-notation">

| Notation                          | Definition and role                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
|:----------------------------------|:------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| $b_i^0,\ g_i^0$                   | Centered amplitude component of the demand lookup $h(B^0)$, divided by $s_j$, and its projection onto $(\mathcal Z,\mathcal R_i,a_i)$; these supply the initial signal.                                                                                           |
| $b_i,\ g_i$                       | Component of the coarse lookup $h_{j-1}(B^j)$ when $i$ is forced shared, and the same conditional projection; Section <a href="#sec:paths" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:paths">7</a> expresses $g_i$ through the common deleted-slot background. |
| $b_i^+$                           | Component with fine smoothing $h_j$ in the same forced experiment; differentiation gives the pointwise and first-moment bounds.                                                                                                                                   |
| $\widetilde b_i,\ \widetilde g_i$ | The clipped component $b_i^+\mathbf 1_{\{U\le B_*,\,v\le B_*\}}$ and its conditional projection; these give bounded quantities for the following norm comparisons.                                                                                                |

Amplitude components. The fine and clipped quantities retain a signal in the coarse projection $g_i$.

</div>

<a id="eq:norm-conversion"></a>
The conditional cap in Equation <a href="#eq:amplitude-signal" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:amplitude-signal">[eq:amplitude-signal]</a>, even with its weaker upper bound one, and Markov’s inequality give $$\|b^0\mathbf 1_{A^c}\|_\Sigma^2
 \le\mathbb P(A^c)\le2(T_0+1)\nu/B_*.$$ Projection contraction, Equation <a href="#eq:component-approximations" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:component-approximations">[eq:component-approximations]</a>, and the triangle inequality now give <a id="eq:clipped-signal-distance"></a><a id="eq:clipped-comparison-distance"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
 \|\widetilde g-g^0\|_\Sigma
 &\le[6\sqrt e+\sqrt{2(T_0+1)/B_*}]\sqrt\nu,
       \label{eq:clipped-signal-distance}\\
 \|\widetilde g-g\|_\Sigma
 &\le[10\sqrt e+\sqrt{2(T_0+1)/B_*}]\sqrt\nu.
       \label{eq:clipped-comparison-distance}
\end{aligned}$$ For every family $q_i$, Equation <a href="#eq:score-scales" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:score-scales">[eq:score-scales]</a> implies $$\label{eq:norm-conversion}
 \frac p2\|q\|_\Sigma^2
 \le\operatorname{avg}_j\mathbb E\operatorname{avg}_{i\in I_j}|q_i|^2
 \le2p\|q\|_\Sigma^2.$$ The first bracket above is at most $\sqrt c/25$ and the second at most $\sqrt c/20$ by Equation <a href="#eq:preconjunction-constants" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:preconjunction-constants">[eq:preconjunction-constants]</a>. Using the amplitude signal and Equation <a href="#eq:norm-conversion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:norm-conversion">[eq:norm-conversion]</a>, we obtain <a id="eq:clipped-signal"></a>$$\label{eq:clipped-signal}
 \operatorname{avg}_j\mathbb E\operatorname{avg}_{i\in I_j}
       |\widetilde g_i|^2\ge c_1p\nu,\qquad
 \operatorname{avg}_j\mathbb E\operatorname{avg}_{i\in I_j}
       |\widetilde g_i-g_i|^2\le\tfrac1{10}c_1p\nu.$$ Also $|\widetilde g_i|\le8B_*$, its averaged first moment is at most $K_*p\nu$ at each stage by Equation <a href="#eq:component-L1" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:component-L1">[eq:component-L1]</a>, and it vanishes when $v>B_*$.

<a id="eq:good-stage-signal"></a>
At most $(T_0+3)/\Delta$ stages are bad. Each contributes at most $8B_*K_*p\nu$ to the unaveraged second moment of $\widetilde g$. Equation <a href="#eq:stage-number" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:stage-number">[eq:stage-number]</a> bounds their average contribution by $c_1p\nu/4$. On $|g_i|<\gamma$, the elementary inequality $$|\widetilde g_i|^2\le2\gamma|\widetilde g_i|
                              +|\widetilde g_i-g_i|^2$$ holds, as follows by expanding the last square and using $|g_i|<\gamma$. Its average is at most $(1/8+1/10)c_1p\nu$. Thus at least $c_1p\nu/2$ of the average second moment remains on good stages with $|g_i|\ge\gamma$ and $v\le B_*$. The bound $|\widetilde g_i|^2\le64B_*^2$ proves that some good stage $j$ satisfies $$\label{eq:good-stage-signal}
 \mathbb P_{\mathcal Z,\,i\ {\rm uniform\ in}\ I_j,\,\mathcal R_i,a_i}
             (|g_i|\ge\gamma,\ v\le B_*)\ge b_*p\nu.$$ We fix this stage for the rest of the analytic argument.

<a id="sec:paths"></a>
### Conditional kernels and ordered paths

Fix the good stage supplied by Equation <a href="#eq:good-stage-signal" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:good-stage-signal">[eq:good-stage-signal]</a>. In this section abbreviate $s=s_j$, $\alpha=\alpha_j$, $\beta=\beta_j$, $I=I_j$, $S=S_j$, and $J=J_{j-1}$. All background experiments are through stage $j$ only. We first identify the conditional laws needed to turn amplitude variation into a common response on predicates.

<a id="pm-h-47"></a>
#### The deleted-slot background

Fix an exterior value $\mathcal Z=z$. It fixes the freeze sets, the complete frozen variables, and the sets $I,S$. Let $Y_i=(\mathcal R_i,a_i)$ for $i\in I$, with common law $P_Y=P_{\mathcal R}\otimes\lambda$. Let $X$ contain all unfrozen primary samples and actual trial variables at stages before $j$, all future primary samples, and the fixed frozen contributions. It determines the preceding partition sequence $C^-=C_{\le j-1}$ and the score $B_{\rm out}$ contributed by slots outside $I$ after earlier stages. It includes no analytic reference gradients or signs as inputs. Actual previous trials used only their preceding query partitions to interpret independent descriptors, so $X$ is independent of the current unfrozen primary samples conditional on $z$.

<a id="eq:conditional-product"></a>
For a preceding partition sequence $C$, write the current trial kernel as <a id="eq:trial-kernel"></a>$$\label{eq:trial-kernel}
 q_C=(1-\alpha)\delta_{\rm shared}
 +\frac\alpha L\sum_{m=1}^L
       P_m(\,\mathrm dE)\operatorname{Unif}(\{-1,+1\}^{J})(\,\mathrm d\tau)
          \operatorname{Unif}[0,1](\,\mathrm du)
          \operatorname{Unif}\{-1,+1\}(\,\mathrm d\chi),$$ where $P_m$ is the fresh data law of mode $m$, including its fresh amplitude if required. The descriptor in this formula is interpreted on $C_{j-1}$. If $T_i$ denotes the current trial, the exact conditional factorization is $$\label{eq:conditional-product}
 P_z(\,\mathrm dX,\,\mathrm dY,\,\mathrm dT)
 =q_z(\,\mathrm dX)\prod_{i\in I}
                  [P_Y(\,\mathrm dY_i)q_{C^-(X)}(\,\mathrm dT_i)].$$ This conditional-on-$X$ product is the independence statement we use. After mixing over $X$, no mutual independence of interpreted trials is needed.

Let $V_C(Y_i,T_i)$ be the current slot summand, including its factor $s$. Then $$B^j_{\neg i}=B_{\rm out}+\sum_{k\in I\setminus\{i\}}V_{C^-}(Y_k,T_k),
 \qquad
 B_\circ^j=B_{\rm out}+\sum_{k\in I\setminus S}V_{C^-}(Y_k,T_k).$$ For every $i\in I$, the pair $(B^j_{\neg i},C^-)$ uses none of $Y_i$. Equation <a href="#eq:conditional-product" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:conditional-product">[eq:conditional-product]</a> shows that its conditional law given $z$ is independent of the entire primary presentation and amplitude in that slot. This law is also the same for every $i\in I$: permutations of these slots preserve the product law and send one deleted sum to another. Denote the common law by $\kappa_z(\,\mathrm dB,\,\mathrm dC)$. The actual fine partition $C_j$ is not a coordinate of this kernel.

<a id="eq:common-amplitude-kernel"></a>
Write $\phi_C(B)$ for lookup smoothed through the preceding partition sequence $C$. In the experiment forcing $i$ shared, put $F_i=\phi_{C^-}(B^j_{\neg i}+sa_ir_i)$, where $r_i$ is the predicate represented by $\mathcal R_i$. The definition of $b_i$ and the tower property give $$s g_i=\mathbb E^{i,\mathrm{sh}}[F_i\mid\mathcal Z,\mathcal R_i,a_i]
              -\mathbb E^{i,\mathrm{sh}}[F_i\mid\mathcal Z,\mathcal R_i].$$ Indeed $\sigma(\mathcal Z,\mathcal R_i,a_i)\subseteq\mathcal B\vee\sigma(a_i)$, and $a_i$ is independent of $\mathcal B$; averaging the subtracted $\mathcal B$-conditional expectation therefore removes $a_i$. Forcing shared preserves the law of the background. The projected response is consequently $$\label{eq:common-amplitude-kernel}
 s\,g_z(r,a)=\int\left[
       \phi_C(B+sar)-\int\phi_C(B+sa'r)\,\mathrm d\lambda(a')
                      \right]\,\mathrm d\kappa_z(B,C).$$ It is a version of every $g_i$, evaluated at the predicate represented by $\mathcal R_i$ and at $a_i$. It depends on the actual predicate $r$, not its presentation. In particular $g_z(0,a)=0$. An unused queried position in a zero-predicate presentation can change $C_j$, but it cannot change Equation <a href="#eq:common-amplitude-kernel" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:common-amplitude-kernel">[eq:common-amplitude-kernel]</a>, which uses only the preceding partitions. Independent sampled predicates may have overlapping bit names; independence here concerns samples and does not require disjoint supports on the assignment cube.

For $i\in S$, extend this background by $\sigma=\operatorname{sign}K_{j-1}(B_\circ^j)$. The triple $(B^j_{\neg i},C^-,\sigma)$ still uses none of $Y_i$ and is unaffected by changing $T_i$. Its conditional law is common to all $i\in S$, by permutations within $S$; call it $\kappa_z^S$. Its $(B,C)$ marginal is precisely $\kappa_z$. This extended independence is asserted only for $i\in S$. The sign can be correlated with the remaining background, and indices outside $S$ can enter its definition.

<a id="eq:coarse-width"></a>
Define the coarse width on any actual predicate $r$ by $$\label{eq:coarse-width}
 F_z(r)=\int\frac{\phi_C(B+s\sigma r)-\phi_C(B-s\sigma r)}s
                       \,\mathrm d\kappa_z^S(B,C,\sigma).$$ Fresh $r$ is independent of this background. Averaging over uniform $i\in S$ in the ordinary experiment is another description of this integral. Because its two amplitude terms ignore $\sigma$, the same extended kernel also represents Equation <a href="#eq:common-amplitude-kernel" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:common-amplitude-kernel">[eq:common-amplitude-kernel]</a>. This supplies one common background for all three values at $ar,-\sigma r,+\sigma r$.

Figure <a href="#fig:conditional-kernels" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:conditional-kernels">1</a> records the two conditional laws and their common marginal.

<figure id="fig:conditional-kernels">

<figcaption>The common coarse backgrounds, conditional on $𝒵 = z$. The displayed laws do not depend on $Y_{i}$; their index ranges differ. Deleting the whole subbatch $S$ in $B_{∘}^{j}$ makes the reference sign independent of each tested $Y_{i}$, conditional on $z$, while allowing dependence on the remaining background. The $(B,C)$ marginal of $κ_{z}^{S}$ is $κ_{z}$, so the amplitude response can also be averaged over the extended kernel. Here $C = C^{−}$, $ϕ_{C}$ uses only preceding smoothing, and the fresh predicate and amplitudes are independent of the background conditional on $z$.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-figsrc-fig-conditional-kernels"></a>
**Figure source and visual witness** (`fig:conditional-kernels`)

- Left box:
  - Upper section titled "Every $i \in I$": displays $(B_{\neg i}^j, C^-) \mid (z, Y_i) \sim \kappa_z$, below which is the text "The current fine partition $C_j$ is absent."
  - Connected by a downward arrow labeled "amplitude response" to a lower section: contains $s\, g_z(r, a) = \mathbb{E}_{\kappa_z} [\phi_C(B + s a r) - \mathbb{E}_{a' \sim \lambda} \phi_C(B + s a' r)]$.
- Right box:
  - Upper section titled "Only $i \in S$": displays $(B_{\neg i}^j, C^-, \sigma) \mid (z, Y_i) \sim \kappa_z^S$ and $\sigma = \operatorname{sign} K_{j-1}(B_\circ^j)$.

  - Connected by a downward arrow labeled "signed width" to a lower section: contains $s\, F_z(r) = \mathbb{E}_{\kappa_z^S} [\phi_C(B + s \sigma r) - \phi_C(B - s \sigma r)]$.
